In addition to\n   372→            # computation/communication overlapping, the strategy allows for\n   373→            # computation/computation overlapping, greatly reducing\n   374→            # quantization inefficiency.\n   375→            #\n   376→            # Ideally, stream activities would look like this (\"b\" for\n   377→            # barriers, \"cp\" for p2p copies):\n   378→            #\n   379→            # [rank 0]\n   380→            # stream 0:         [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer ][b][ cp ]\n   381→            # stream 1: [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ]\n   382→            #\n   383→            # [rank 1]\n   384→            # stream 0:         [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer ][b][ cp ]\n   385→            # stream 1: [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ]\n   386→            #\n   387→            # Note that the barriers synchronize streams with the same ID\n   388→            # across ranks. They don't synchronize streams on the same rank.\n   389→            #\n   390→            # Since the work on both streams is independent, there's no\n   391→            # guarantee that the chunk_producer from stream 0 or stream 1 will\n   392→            # be scheduled first. If there is a scheduling mismatch across\n   393→            # ranks, the barrier forces all ranks to wait for the slowest.\n   394→            #\n   395→            # When scheduling mismatches occur among ranks, the stream\n   396→            # activities might look like this (note that p2p copies from\n   397→            # different streams cannot overlap with each other):\n   398→            #\n   399→            # [rank 0]\n   400→            # stream 0: [  chunk_producer  ][b        ][ cp ][  chunk_producer ][b       ][ cp ]\n   401→            # stream 1:         [  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ]\n   402→            #\n   403→            # [rank 1]\n   404→            # stream 0:         [  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ]\n   405→            # stream 1: [  chunk_producer  ][b        ][ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b      ][ cp ]\n   406→            #\n   407→            # To prevent this, we need to ensure that the chunk_producer on\n   408→            # stream 1 gets scheduled first on every rank. Without access to\n   409→            # the underlying kernels, CUDA offers no API to control the\n   410→            # scheduling order of two independent, overlapping kernels. Our\n   411→            # solution is to issue a small sleep kernel in stream 0. The sleep\n   412→            # duration is insignificant, but having an extra task in stream 0\n   413→            # will almost guarantee that the chunk_producer on stream 1 gets\n   414→            # scheduled first. Once the first chunk_producer is scheduled in\n   415→            # the correct order, there's very little room for the scheduling\n   416→            # order of subsequent kernels to be inconsistent across ranks.\n   417→            if step == 2:\n   418→                torch.cuda._sleep(100)\n   419→            chunk_producer((rank + step) % group_size, p2p_buf)\n   420→            symm_mem.barrier(channel=step % 2)\n   421→            out_chunks[remote_rank].copy_(remote_p2p_buf)\n   422→            # The local P2P buffer can only be overwritten by the next\n   423→            # chunk_producer after all peers have finished reading from it.\n   424→            symm_mem.barrier(channel=step % 2)\n   425→\n   426→    # The returned tensors are ordered by rank IDs. The\n   474→length of the tuple equals to the size of the group.\n   475→\n   476→Note: this API works only when `world_within_direct_access()` returns True, i.e.\n   477→only when the group is within NVLink domain or similar.        shard=A_shard, scale=A_scale, gather_dim=gather_dim, group_size=group.size()\n   600→    )\n   601→\n   602→    # Computing block-wise matmul along the first dim of A\n   603→    if scale_mode 699→            shard.dtype,\n   700→        )\n   701→        return buf\n   702→\n   703→    local_p2p_buf = get_p2p_buf(rank)\n   704→\n   705→    shards = ag_out.chunk(group_size)\n   706→\n torch.ops.symm_mem._async_input_mm(A, B, A_signals, rank)\n   951→    for step in range(1, world_size):\n   952→        src_rank = (rank + step) % world_size\n   953→        src_buf = _SymmetricMemory.stream_write_value32(A_signals, src_rank, 1)\n   959→            else:\n   960→                _SymmetricMemory.memset32(A_signals, offset=src_rank, val=1, count=1)\n   961→\n   962→ _should_use_multimem_all_gather_matmul(\n   970→    A_shard: torch.Tensor,\n   971→    gather_dim: int,\n   972→    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n   973→    return_A: bool,\n   974→) -> bool:\n   975→ We could refine it with a more\n   990→        # sophisticated perf model.\n   991→        and local_M * group.size() <= 2048\n   992→    )\n   993→\n   994→\n   995→def _multimem_all_gather_matmul(\n 1170→    Restride `t` such that `t.permute(perm)` is contiguous.\n  1171→    \"\"\"\n  1172→    inv_perm = [0] * len(perm)\n  1173→    for i, p in enumerate(perm):\n  1174→        inv_perm[p] = i\n use_fast_accum,\n  1418→    )\n  1419→    C = C.view(*output_shape[:-1], B.shape[1])\n  1420→    res = funcol.reduce_scatter_tensor(\n  1421→        C,\n  1422→        reduce_op,\n  1423→        orig_scatter_dim,  # need original scatter dim for 3D+ output tensor here\n  1424→        group_name,\n  1425→    )\n  1426→    res = funcol.wait_tensor(res)\n  1427→    return res\n  1428→\n  1429→\n  1430→def _fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter_impl(\n  1431→    mm_out_op: torch._ops.OpOverload,\n  1432→    A: torch.Tensor,\n  1433→    B: torch.Tensor,\n  1434→    A_scale: torch.Tensor,\n  1435→    kwargs: dict[str, Any],\n  1436→    out_dtype: torch.dtype | None,\n  1437→    reduce_op: str,\n  1438→    orig_scatter_dim: int,\n  1439→    scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape: int,\n  1440→    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n  1441→    output_shape: list[int],\n  1442→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1443→    if A.dim() < 2:\n  1444→        raise ValueError(\"A_shard must be a matrix\")\n  1445→    if (\n  1446→        scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape < 0\n  1447→        or scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape >= A.dim()\n  1448→    ):\n  1449→        raise ValueError(\"Invalid scatter dim for 2D tensor input to scaled_mm\")\n  1450→    if orig_scatter_dim < 0 or orig_scatter_dim >= len(output_shape):\n  1451→        raise ValueError(\"Invalid scatter dim for 3D+ output tensor\")\n  1452→    if B.dim() != 2:\n  1453→        raise ValueError(\"B must be a matrix\")\n  1454→    if reduce_op == \"sum\":\n  1455→        reduce_fn = partial(torch.sum, dim=0)\n  1456→    elif reduce_op == \"avg\":\n  1457→        reduce_fn = partial(torch.mean, dim=0)\n  1458→    else:\n  1459→        raise ValueError(\"reduce_op must be sum or avg\")\n  1460→\n  1461→    group = c10d._resolve_process_group(group_name)\n  1462→\n  1463→    # Move scatter to first dim, then shard the tensor along the first dim, so the chunk producer\n  1464→    # can perform matmuls along the first dim.\n  1465→    A_with_scatter_dim_0 = A.movedim(scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape, 0)\n  1466→\n  1467→    # To handle case where A is 3D+, reshape to 2D to prepare for mm which requires 2D inputs.\n  1468→    A_2D_with_scatter_dim_0 = A_with_scatter_dim_0.flatten(0, -2)\n  1469→\n  1470→    # Partition A along the first dim to prepare for sharding across TP process group.\n  1471→    A_shards = A_2D_with_scatter_dim_0.chunk(group.size())\n  1472→\n  1473→    # Now that 'A' is sharded along the first dim, we need to update its scale(s) accordingly.\n  1474→    # How we do this depends on if we are using tensorwise scaling, rowwise scaling, or no scaling.\n  1475→    tensorwise_scaling = A_scale is not None and A_scale.numel() == 1\n  1476→    rowwise_scaling = A_scale is not None and A_scale.numel() > 1\n  1477→\n  1478→    # For tensorwise scaling, the scale should be replicated so each shard has a copy.\n  1479→    if tensorwise_scaling:\n  1480→        A_scale_shards = [A_scale] * group.size()\n  1481→\n  1482→    # For rowwise scaling, we need to move the scatter dim to the first dim to match the\n  1483→    # dim swap of the 'A' tensor. Then we can shard the scales along the first dim, just like\n  1484→    # the 'A' tensor.\n  1485→    elif rowwise_scaling:\n  1486→        if A_scale.shape[:-1] != A.shape[:-1]:\n  1487→            raise ValueError(\n  1488→                \"For row-wise scaling, the leading dims of A_scale \"\n  1489→                \"must match the leading dims of A \"\n  1490→                f\"(A shape: {A.shape}, A_scale shape: {A_scale.shape})\"\n  1491→            )\n  1492→        A_scale = (\n  1493→            A_scale.movedim(scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape, 0)\n  1494→            .contiguous()\n  1495→            .flatten(0, -2)\n  1496→        )\n  1497→        A_scale_shards = list(A_scale.chunk(group.size()))\n  1498→        # cuBLAS's row-wise kernel requires scales to be aligned to 16 bytes.\n  1499→        # When we slice them we might break this and need to reallocate them.\n  1500→        A_scale_shards = [\n  1501→            t if t.data_ptr() % 16 == 0 else t.clone() for t in A_scale_shards\n  1502→        ]\n  1503→    else:\n  1504→        raise ValueError(\"A_scale cannot be none for scaled_mm\")\n  1505→\n  1506→    # Computing block-wise matmul along the first dim of A\n  1507→    def chunk_producer(rank: int, out: torch.Tensor) -> None:\n  1508→        mm_out_op(A_shards[rank], B, scale_a=A_scale_shards[rank], **kwargs, out=out)\n  1509→\n  1510→    # Stacked partials will be the 2D outputs of the pipelined scaled mm, and will\n  1511→    # have the shape (A_with_scatter_dim_0_tensor.shape[0], B.shape[1]) to align with the formula:\n  1512→    # (a*b,c) @ (c,d) = (a*b,d)\n  1513→    stacked_partials = A_with_scatter_dim_0.new_empty(\n  1514→        A_2D_with_scatter_dim_0.shape[0], B.shape[1], dtype=out_dtype or A.dtype\n  1515→    )\n  1516→\n  1517→    # Execute the pipelined mm/scaled_mm.\n  1518→    _pipelined_produce_and_all2all(\n  1519→        chunk_producer,\n  1520→        stacked_partials,\n  1521→        group_name,\n  1522→    )\n  1523→\n  1524→    # We now need to transform the *unreduced* stacked 2D partial mm outputs to an *unreduced* 3D+ output,\n  1525→    # then reduce-scatter. To do this, we first need to determine the shape of the unreduced 3D+ output,\n  1526→    # to reshape our stacked partials so we can apply the reduce-scatter.\n  1527→    #\n  1528→    # The *unreduced* 3D+ tensor will have dim 0 = `group_size`, as we have `group_size` instances of\n  1529→    # stacked partial outputs. The next dims will be A's leading dims (sharded along the original scatter dim),\n  1530→    # as it was the left operand of the mm op. We can use -1 as the final dim of the view to populate the rest.\n  1531→    stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims = [group.size()] + list(\n  1532→        # We use A from after the dim swap 0<=>scatter_dim, but before the flatten,\n  1533→        # to get the leading dims of the 3D+ view of stacked partials.\n  1534→        A_with_scatter_dim_0.shape[:-1]\n  1535→    )\n  1536→\n  1537→    # The `group_size` leading dim has been prepended to `stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims`,\n  1538→    # to capture the partial output from each rank. We need to divide the sharding/scatter dim\n  1539→    # by the group size. If the original scatter dim was 0, then it is now dim 1 in this\n  1540→    # tensor, since this new `group_size` dim was prepended.\n  1541→    stacked_partial_scatter_dim = orig_scatter_dim if orig_scatter_dim > 0 else 1\n  1542→    stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims[stacked_partial_scatter_dim] //= group.size()\n  1543→\n  1544→    # Ensures that the transpose and reduction produce contiguous result\n  1545→    # in a single reduction kernel.\n  1546→    reduced_out = reduce_fn(\n  1547→        # View 2D stacked partials as 3D+ tensor of shape (`group_size`, ...)\n  1548→        stacked_partials.view(*stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims, -1)\n  1549→        # We originally swapped 0<=>scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape. Now after\n  1550→        # prepending the `group_size` dim, to undo this original swap, we\n  1551→        # must swap 1<=>scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape+1.\n  1552→        .movedim(1, scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape + 1),\n  1553→        # Reduce along the `group_size` dim (0).\n  1554→        dim=0,\n  1555→    )\n  1556→\n  1557→    # Output shape must be scattered along original scatter dim as well.\n  1558→    output_shape[orig_scatter_dim] //= group.size()\n  1559→    out = reduced_out.view(*output_shape)\n  1560→    return out\n  1561→\n  1562→\n  1563→def restride_A_for_fused_matmul_reduce_scatter(\n  1564→    t: torch.Tensor,\n  1565→    scatter_dim: int,\n  1566→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1567→    \"\"\"\n  1568→    Restride the `A_shard` arg of `fused_matmul_reduce_scatter` for optimal\n  1569→    perf. See the doc for `fused_matmul_reduce_scatter` for detail.\n  1570→    \"\"\"\n  1571→    perm = list(range(len(t.shape)))\n  1572→    perm.insert(0, perm.pop(scatter_dim))\n  1573→    return make_contiguous_for_perm(t, perm)\n  1574→\n  1575→\n  1576→def _maybe_convert_scalar_types_to_dtypes(\n  1577→    scalar_types: list[Any],\n  1578→) -> list[torch.dtype | None]:\n  1579→    \"\"\"\n  1580→    When a list of `torch.dtype`s is passed through the dispatcher as\n  1581→    `ScalarType[]`, it is converted to a list of scalar type enum values. This\n  1582→    function converts it back to a list of `torch.dtype`s.\n  1583→    \"\"\"\n  1584→    # Order defined in https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/344defc9733a45fee8d0c4d3f5530f631e823196/c10/core/ScalarType.h\n  1585→    _SCALAR_TYPE_TO_DTYPE = {\n  1586→        0: torch.uint8,\n  1587→        1: torch.int8,\n  1588→        2: torch.short,\n  1589→        3: torch.int,\n  1590→        4: torch.int64,\n  1591→        5: torch.half,\n  1592→        6: torch.float,\n  1593→        7: torch.double,\n  1594→        8: torch.complex32,\n  1595→        9: torch.complex64,\n  1596→        10: torch.complex128,\n  1597→        11: torch.bool,\n  1598→        12: torch.qint8,\n  1599→        13: torch.quint8,\n  1600→        14: torch.qint32,\n  1601→        15: torch.bfloat16,\n  1602→        16: torch.float8_e5m2,\n  1603→        17: torch.float8_e4m3fn,\n  1604→        18: torch.float8_e5m2fnuz,\n  1605→        19: torch.float8_e4m3fnuz,\n  1606→    }\n  1607→    if any(not isinstance(x, (type(None), int)) for x in scalar_types):\n  1608→        return scalar_types\n  1609→\n  1610→    dtypes: list[torch.dtype | None] = []\n  1611→    for scalar_type in scalar_types:\n  1612→        if scalar_type is None:\n  1613→            dtypes.append(scalar_type)\n  1614→        elif scalar_type not in _SCALAR_TYPE_TO_DTYPE:\n  1615→            raise ValueError(f\"Unrecognized scalar type {scalar_type}\")\n  1616→        else:\n  1617→            dtypes.append(_SCALAR_TYPE_TO_DTYPE[scalar_type])\n  1618→    return dtypes\n  1619→\n  1620→\n  1621→class Work(_Work):\n  1622→    def __init__(self) -> None:\n  1623→        super().__init__()\n  1624→        self.event = torch.cuda.Event()\n  1625→        self.event.record()\n  1626→\n  1627→    def wait(self, timeout: timedelta = timedelta(seconds=0)) -> bool:\n  1628→        self.event.wait()\n  1629→        return True\n  1630→\n  1631→\n  1632→\"\"\"\n  1633→NOTE [low-contention collectives]\n  1634→When a collective is overlapped with abundant compute, it makes sense to\n  1635→prioritize reducing the contention between the collective and the overlapped\n  1636→compute, even at the cost of a slightly slower collective.\n  1637→\n  1638→Common collective implementations (e.g., NCCL without user buffer\n  1639→registration) optimize for throughput with no ambient compute. However, such\n  1640→implementations may not be optimal when they are overlapped with compute:\n  1641→- These implementations typically fuse the entire collective into a single\n  1642→kernel and reserve SM resources based on the most demanding portion of the\n  1643→collective, even when a large portion of the collective does not require this\n  1644→much resource.\n  1645→- These implementations often use SM-based P2P copy as opposed to copy\n  1646→engine-based P2P copy. Copy engine-based P2P copy may not have a significant\n  1647→advantage when there's no ambient compute. However, it may significantly\n  1648→improve overall resource utilization in the presence of ambient compute.\n  1649→\n  1650→When overlapped with intensive compute (e.g., persistent matmul kernels), the\n  1651→SM-usage of a collective can lead to inefficient overlapping.\n  1652→\n  1653→Low-contention collectives achieve their goals with the following strategies:\n  1654→- Use copy engine-based copy whenever possible.\n  1655→- Break down portions of a collective with different resource requirements\n  1656→into multiple kernels. This improves the overlapping efficiency at the cost\n  1657→of additional launching overhead.\n  1658→\"\"\"\n  1659→\n  1660→\n  1661→@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_all_gather\", \"Meta\")\n  1662→def _low_contention_all_gather_meta(\n  1663→    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n  1664→    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n  1665→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1666→    group_size = c10d._get_group_size_by_name(group_name)\n  1667→    return tensor.new_empty(tensor.shape[0] * group_size, *tensor.shape[1:])\n  1668→\n  1669→\n  1670→@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_all_gather\", \"CUDA\")\n  1671→def _low_contention_all_gather(\n  1672→    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n  1673→    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n  1674→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1675→    \"\"\"\n  1676→    Performs all-gather with symmetric memory in a low-contention fashion.\n  1677→\n  1678→    When `tensor` is already in symmetric memory:\n  1679→        - The collective is carried out without using SMs.\n  1680→        - No symmetric memory workspace is required.\n  1681→\n  1682→    When `tensor` is not in symmetric memory:\n  1683→        - An extra SM-based copy is performed to copy the input data into the\n  1684→          symmetric memory workspace.\n  1685→        - Symmetric memory workspace size requirement: the size of `tensor`.\n  1686→    \"\"\"\n  1687→    symm_mem = rendezvous(tensor, group_name)\n  1688→    if symm_mem is not None:\n  1689→        input_is_symm_mem = True\n  1690→    else:\n  1691→        symm_mem = get_symm_mem_workspace(\n  1692→            group_name, tensor.numel() * tensor.element_size()\n  1693→        )\n  1694→        input_is_symm_mem = False\n  1695→\n  1696→    rank = symm_mem.rank\n  1697→    world_size = symm_mem.world_size\n  1698→\n  1699→    output = tensor.new_empty(tensor.shape[0] * world_size, *tensor.shape[1:])\n  1700→    chunks = output.chunk(world_size)\n  1701→\n  1702→    _get_backend_stream().wait_stream(torch.cuda.current_stream())\n  1703→    with _get_backend_stream():\n  1704→        if not input_is_symm_mem:\n  1705→            local_buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(rank, tensor.shape, tensor.dtype)\n  1706→            local_buf.copy_(tensor)\n  1707→        # pull\n  1708→        symm_mem.barrier()\n  1709→        for step in range(world_size):\n  1710→            remote_rank = (rank - step) % world_size\n  1711→            src_buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(remote_rank, tensor.shape, tensor.dtype)\n  1712→            chunks[remote_rank].copy_(src_buf)\n  1713→        symm_mem.barrier()\n  1714→        torch._C._distributed_c10d._register_work(output, Work())\n  1715→        return output\n  1716→\n  1717→\n  1718→@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_reduce_scatter\", \"Meta\")\n  1719→def _low_contention_reduce_scatter_meta(\n  1720→    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n  1721→    reduce_op: str,\n  1722→    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n  1723→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1724→    group_size = c10d._get_group_size_by_name(group_name)\n  1725→    return tensor.unflatten(0, (group_size, -1)).mean(dim=0)\n  1726→\n  1727→\n  1728→def _low_contention_reduce_scatter_with_symm_mem_input(\n  1729→    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n  1730→    reduce_op: str,\n  1731→    symm_mem: _SymmetricMemory,\n  1732→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1733→    rank = symm_mem.rank\n  1734→    world_size = symm_mem.world_size\n  1735→\n  1736→    assert tensor.shape[0] % world_size == 0\n  1737→    a2a_res = 1742→        # pull + offline reduction\n  1743→        symm_mem.barrier()\n  1744→        for step in range(world_size):\n  1745→            remote_rank = (rank - step) % world_size\n  1746→            src_buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(\n  1747→                remote_rank,\n  1748→                chunks[0].shape,\n  1749→                chunks[0].dtype,\n  1750→                chunks[0].numel() *    if reduce_op == \"sum\":\n  1757→            ret = ret.sum(dim=0)\n  1758→        elif reduce_op == \"avg\":\n  1759→            ret = ret.mean(dim=0)\n  1760→        else:\n  1761→            raise ValueError(f\"reduce_op ({reduce_op}) is not supported\")\n  1762→        torch._C._distributed_c10d._register_work(ret, Work())\n  1763→        return ret\n  1764→\n  1765→\n  1766→def _low_contention_reduce_scatter_with_workspace(\n  1767→    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n  1768→    reduce_op: str,\n  1769→    workspace: _SymmetricMemory,\n  1770→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1771→    rank = workspace.rank\n  1772→    world_size = workspace.world_size\n  1773→\n  1774→    assert tensor.shape[0] % world_size == 0\n  1775→    chunks = tensor.chunk(world_size)\n  1776→\n  1777→    _get_backend_stream().wait_stream(torch.cuda.current_stream())\n  1778→    with _get_backend_stream():\n  1779→        # push + offline reduction\n  1780→        workspace.barrier()\n  1781→        for step in range(world_size):\n  1782→            remote_rank = (rank - step) % world_size\n  1783→            dst_buf = workspace.get_buffer(\n  1784→                remote_rank, chunks[0].shape, chunks[0].dtype, chunks[0].numel() * rank\n  1785→            )\n  1786→            dst_buf.copy_(chunks[remote_rank])\n  1787→        workspace.barrier()\n  1788→\n  1789→        buf = 1793→        elif reduce_op == \"avg\":\n  1794→            ret = ret.mean(dim=0)\n  1795→        else:\n  1796→            raise ValueError(f\"reduce_op ({reduce_op}) is not supported\")\n  1797→        torch._C._distributed_c10d._register_work(ret, Work())\n  1798→        return ret\n  1799→\n  1800→\n  1801→@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_reduce_scatter\", \"CUDA\")\n  1802→def _low_contention_reduce_scatter(\n  1803→    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n  1804→    reduce_op: str,\n  1805→    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n  1806→) -> torch.Tensor:\n  1807→    \"\"\"\n  1808→    Performs 1813→    When `tensor` is already in symmetric memory:\n  1814→        - Pull-based all-to-all is used.\n  1815→        - No symmetric memory workspace is required.\n  1816→\n  1817→    When `tensor` is not in symmetric memory:\n  1818→        - Push-based all-to-all is used.\n  1819→        - Symmetric memory workspace size requirement: the size of `tensor`.\n  1820→\n  1821→    SM-usage:\n  1822→        - SM-based copy of the rank's own chunk for the all-to-all.\n  1823→        - Reduction on the all-to-all result.\n  1824→\n  1825→    TODO(yifu): the SM-based copy can be avoided with a list-based reduction\n  1826→    kernel.\n  1827→    \"\"\"\n  1828→    symm_mem = rendezvous(tensor, group_name)\n  1829→    if symm_mem is not None:\n  1830→        return        group_name, tensor.numel() * tensor.element_size()\n  1836→        )\n  1837→        return _low_contention_reduce_scatter_with_workspace(\n  1838→            tensor, reduce_op, workspace\n  1839→        )\n  1840→\n  1841→\n  1842→@torch.library.impl(lib, \"all_to_all_vdev_2d\", \"Meta\")\n  1843→def _all_to_all_vdev_2d_meta(\n  1844→    input: torch.Tensor,\n  1845→    out: p2p copy -> shard_consumer\n    # sequences to two streams. In addition to computation/communication\n    # overlapping, the strategy allows for computation/computation overlapping,\n    # greatly reducing quantization inefficiency.\n    #\n    # Notation:\n    # - \"mv\" for the copy to local buffer\n    # - \"cp\" for p2p copies\n    # - \"b\" for barriers\n    #\n    # Constraints:\n    # - The GPU scheduler may or may not overlap \"mv\" with the first shard_consumer.\n    # - \"cp\" from different streams cannot overlap.\n    #\n    # Ideal scenario 0 - \"mv\" overlaps with the first shard_consumer:\n    #\n    # stream 0: [ shard_consumer ][ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    # stream 1: [ mv ][b][ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    #\n    # Ideal scenario 1 - \"mv\" is scheduled before the first shard_consumer:\n    #\n    # stream 0:       [ shard_consumer ][ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    # stream 1: [ mv ][b][ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    #\n    # Suboptimal scenario 0 - \"mv\" is scheduled after the first shard_consumer:\n    #\n    # stream 0: [ shard_consumer ]               [ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    # stream 1:                   [ mv ][b][ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    #\n    # Suboptimal scenario 0 - \"b\" is scheduled after the first shard_consumer:\n    #\n    # stream 0:       [ shard_consumer ]         [ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    # stream 1: [ mv ]                  [b][ cp ][ shard_consumer ]\n    #\n    # We haven't yet figured out a way to ensure \"mv\" and \"b\" are either\n    # overlapped with or scheduled before the first shard_consumer. p2p_buf = local_p2p_buf_0\n            remote_p2p_buf = get_p2p_buf(remote_rank, 0)\n        with stream:\n            # Parallelization strategy: every rank issues independent compute\n            # -> barrier -> p2p copy sequences on two streams. In addition to\n            # computation/communication overlapping, the strategy allows for\n            # computation/computation overlapping, greatly reducing\n            # quantization inefficiency.\n            #\n            # Ideally, stream activities would look like this (\"b\" for\n            # barriers, \"cp\" for p2p copies):\n            #\n            # [rank 0]\n            # stream 0:         [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer ][b][ cp ]\n            # stream 1: [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ]\n            #\n            # [rank 1]\n            # stream 0:         [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer ][b][ cp ]\n            # stream 1: [  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b][ cp ]\n            #\n            # Note that the barriers synchronize streams with the same ID\n            # across ranks. They don't synchronize streams on the same rank.\n            #\n            # Since the work on both streams is independent, there's no\n            # guarantee that the chunk_producer from stream 0 or stream 1 will\n            # be scheduled first. If there is a scheduling mismatch across\n            # ranks, the barrier forces all ranks to wait for the slowest.\n            #\n            # When scheduling mismatches occur among ranks, the stream\n            # activities might look like this (note that p2p copies from\n            # different streams cannot overlap with each other):\n            #\n            # [rank 0]\n            # stream 0: [  chunk_producer  ][b        ][ cp ][  chunk_producer ][b       ][ cp ]\n            # stream 1:         [  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ]\n            #\n            # [rank 1]\n            # stream 0:         [  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b]      [ cp ]\n            # stream 1: [  chunk_producer  ][b        ][ cp ][  chunk_producer  ][b      ][ cp ]\n            #\n            # To prevent this, we need to ensure that the chunk_producer on\n            # stream 1 gets scheduled first on every rank. Without access to\n            # the underlying kernels, CUDA offers no API to control the\n            # scheduling order of two independent, overlapping kernels. Our\n            # solution is to issue a small sleep kernel in stream 0. The sleep\n            # duration is insignificant, but having an extra task in stream 0\n            # will almost guarantee that the chunk_producer on stream 1 gets\n            # scheduled first. Once the first chunk_producer is scheduled in\n            # the correct order, there's very little room for the scheduling\n            # order of subsequent kernels to be inconsistent across ranks.\n            if step == 2:\n                torch.cuda._sleep(100)\n            chunk_producer((rank + step) % group_size, p2p_buf)\n            symm_mem.barrier(channel=step % 2)\n            out_chunks[remote_rank].copy_(remote_p2p_buf)\n            # The local P2P buffer can only be overwritten by the next\n            # chunk_producer after all peers have finished reading from it.\n            symm_mem.barrier(channel=step % 2)\n\n    # If the sleep wasn't issued in the above loop, do it now.\n    if group_size == 2:\n        torch.cuda._sleep(100)\n\n    chunk_producer(rank, out_chunks[rank])\n    torch.cuda.current_stream().wait_stream(backend_stream)\n    symm_mem.barrier(channel=0)\n\n\nlib = torch.library.Library(\"symm_mem\", \"DEF\")  # noqa: TOR901\nlib.define(\n    \"fused_all_gather_matmul(\"\n    \"Tensor A, Tensor[] Bs, int gather_dim, str group_name, *, bool return_A = True) -> (Tensor?, Tensor[])\",\n    tags=[torch._C.Tag.needs_fixed_stride_order],\n)\nlib.define(\n    \"fused_all_gather_scaled_matmul(\"\n    \"Tensor A, Tensor[] Bs, Tensor A_scale, Tensor[] B_scales, \"\n    \"int gather_dim, str group_name, \"\n    \"Tensor?[] biases, \"\n    \"Tensor?[] result_scales, \"\n    \"ScalarType?[] out_dtypes, \"\n    \"bool[] use_fast_accum) -> (Tensor, Tensor[])\",\n    tags=[torch._C.Tag.needs_fixed_stride_order],\n)\nlib.define(\n    \"fused_matmul_reduce_scatter(Tensor A, Tensor B, str reduce_op, int scatter_dim, str group_name) -> Tensor\",\n    tags=[torch._C.Tag.needs_fixed_stride_order],\n)\nlib.define(\n    \"fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter(\"\n    \"Tensor A, Tensor B, Tensor A_scale, Tensor B_scale, \"\n    \"str reduce_op, int orig_scatter_dim, int scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape, str group_name, SymInt[]? output_shape, \"\n    \"Tensor? bias = None, \"\n    \"Tensor? result_scale = None, \"\n    \"ScalarType? out_dtype = None, \"\n    \"bool use_fast_accum = False) -> Tensor\",\n    tags=[torch._C.Tag.needs_fixed_stride_order],\n)\nlib.define(\"_low_contention_all_gather(Tensor tensor, str group_name) -> Tensor\")\nlib.define(\n    \"_low_contention_reduce_scatter(Tensor tensor, str reduce_op, str group_name) -> Tensor\"\n)\n\nlib.define(\"get_remote_tensors(Tensor x, str group_name) -> Tensor[]\")\n\"\"\"\nGiven a local tensor and a group name, return a tuple of tensors that are\nsymmetric on other devices. The returned tensors are ordered by rank IDs. The\nlength of the tuple equals to the size of the group.\n\nNote: this API works only when `world_within_direct_access()` returns True, i.e.\nonly when the group is within NVLink domain or similar. It does not work across\nnetwork interfaces.\n\"\"\"\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"get_remote_tensors\", \"CUDA\")\ndef _get_remote_tensors_default(\n    local: torch.Tensor, group_name: c10d.GroupName\n) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, ...]:\n    hdl = rendezvous(local, group_name)\n    if hdl is None:\n        raise ValueError(\"Tensor is not allocated from Symmetric Memory\")\n\n    return tuple(\n        hdl.get_remote_tensor(peer, local.size(), local.dtype)\n        for peer in range(hdl.world_size)\n    )\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"get_remote_tensors\", \"Meta\")\ndef _get_remote_tensors_meta(\n    local: torch.Tensor, group_name: c10d.GroupName\n) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, ...]:\n    group = c10d._resolve_process_group(group_name)\n    return tuple(torch.empty_like(local) for _ in range(group.size()))\n\n\nclass _ScaleMode(Enum):\n    UNSCALED = \"unscaled\"\n    TENSOR_WISE = \"tensor-wise\"\n    ROW_WISE_SHARDED = \"row-wise-sharded\"\n    ROW_WISE_REPLICATED = \"row-wise-replicated\"\n\n\ndef _check_and_verify_fp8_all_gather_scale_mode(\n    shard: torch.Tensor, scale: torch.Tensor | None, gather_dim: int, group_size: int\n) -> _ScaleMode:\n    full_shape = list(shard.shape)\n    full_shape[gather_dim] *= group_size\n\n    if scale is None:\n        return _ScaleMode.UNSCALED\n    elif scale.shape[:-1] == shard.shape[:-1] and scale.shape[-1] == 1:\n        # Row-wise scaling\n        #\n        # NOTE: when the last dim of both A_shard and A_scale is one, we can't\n        # tell if A_scale is replicated tensor-wise scale or sharded row-wise\n        # scale. Treating it as row-wise scaling for safety.\n        return _ScaleMode.ROW_WISE_SHARDED\n    elif scale.numel() == 1:\n        return _ScaleMode.TENSOR_WISE\n    elif list(scale.shape[:-1]) == full_shape[:-1]:\n        return _ScaleMode.ROW_WISE_REPLICATED\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\n            \"Invalid scale shape for fp8 all-gather \"\n            f\"(shard shape: {shard.shape}, scale shape: {scale.shape})\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _fused_all_gather_matmul_impl(\n    mm_out_op: torch._ops.OpOverload,\n    A_shard: torch.Tensor,\n    Bs: list[torch.Tensor],\n    A_scale: torch.Tensor | None,\n    kwargs_list: list[dict[str, Any]],\n    out_dtypes: list[torch.dtype | None],\n    gather_dim: int,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n    return_A: bool,\n) -> tuple[torch.Tensor | None, list[torch.Tensor]]:\n    if A_shard.dim() < 2:\n        raise ValueError(\"A_shard must be a matrix\")\n    for B in Bs:\n        if B.dim() != 2:\n            raise ValueError(\"len(kwargs_list) must be the same as len(Bs)\")\n    if gather_dim < 0 or gather_dim >= A_shard.dim():\n        raise ValueError(\"Invalid gather_dim\")\n\n    group = c10d._resolve_process_group(group_name)\n\n    if gather_dim == A_shard.ndim - 1 or gather_dim == -1:\n        return _fused_all_gather_matmul_last_gather_dim_impl(\n            mm_out_op,\n            A_shard,\n            Bs,\n            A_scale,\n            kwargs_list,\n            out_dtypes,\n            gather_dim,\n            group_name,\n            return_A,\n        )\n\n    # Move the gather_dim to the front and flatten the tensor into a 2D matrix.\n    # The flattened tensor doesn't need to be contiguous (for computation\n    # efficiency), as _pipelined_all_gather_and_consume A_flat.new_empty(A_flat.shape[0], B.shape[1], dtype=out_dtype or B.dtype)\n        for B, out_dtype in zip(Bs, out_dtypes)\n    ]\n    output_shards = [output.chunk(group.size()) for output in              scale_a=shard[1],\n                    **kwargs,\n                    out=output_shards[idx][rank],\n                )\n\n        _pipelined_multi_all_gather_and_consume(\n row_wise_replicated_consumer(shard: torch.Tensor, rank: int) -> None:\n            for idx, (B, kwargs) in enumerate(zip(Bs, kwargs_list)):\n                mm_out_op(\n                    shard,\n     if scale_mode == _ScaleMode.TENSOR_WISE:\n            assert A_scale is not None\n            for kwargs in kwargs_list:\n                kwargs[\"scale_a\"] = A_scale\n        else:\n mm_out_op(shard, B, **kwargs, out=output_shards[idx][rank])\n\n        _pipelined_all_gather_and_consume(\n            A_shard_flat,\n            default_consumer,\n            A_flat,\n            group_name,\n            return_A,\n        )\n\n    A = unflatten(A_flat) if return_A else None\n    return A, [unflatten(output) for output in outputs]\n\n\ndef _pipelined_all_gather_and_consume_last_dim(\n    shard: torch.Tensor,\n    shard_consumer: Callable[[torch.Tensor, int], None],\n    ag_out: torch.Tensor,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n    ag_out_needed: bool = True,\n) -> None:\n    p2p_workspace_size_req = 0\n    p2p_workspace_size_req = shard.numel() * shard.element_size()\n    symm_mem = get_symm_mem_workspace(group_name, min_size=p2p_workspace_size_req)\n    group_size = symm_mem.world_size\n    rank = symm_mem.rank\n\n    symm_mem.barrier(channel=0)\n    backend_stream = _get_backend_stream()\n    backend_stream.wait_stream(torch.cuda.current_stream())\n\n    def copy_shard(dst: torch.Tensor, src: torch.Tensor) -> None:\n        dst.copy_(src)\n\n    def get_p2p_buf(remote_rank: int) -> torch.Tensor:\n        buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(\n            remote_rank,\n            shard.shape,\n            shard.dtype,\n        )\n        return buf\n\n    local_p2p_buf = get_p2p_buf(rank)\n\n    shards = ag_out.chunk(group_size)\n\n    copy_shard(dst=local_p2p_buf, src=shard)\n    symm_mem.barrier(channel=1)\n _fused_all_gather_matmul_last_gather_dim_impl(\n    mm_out_op: torch._ops.OpOverload,\n    A_shard: torch.Tensor,\n    Bs: list[torch.Tensor],\n    A_scale: torch.Tensor | None,\n    kwargs_list:    with torch.profiler.record_function(\"fused_matmul_reduce_scatter\"):\n        return _fused_matmul_reduce_scatter_impl(\n            mm_out_op=torch.ops.aten.mm.out,\n            A=A,\n reduce_op: str,\n    scatter_dim: int,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    if A.dim() < 2:\n        raise ValueError(\"A_shard must be a matrix\")\n    if scatter_dim < 0 or scatter_dim >= A.dim():\n        raise ValueError(\"Invalid gather_dim\")\n    if B.dim() != 2:\n        raise ValueError(\"B must be a matrix\")\n    if reduce_op == \"sum\":\n        reduce_fn = partial(torch.sum, dim=0)\n    elif reduce_op == \"avg\":\n        reduce_fn = partial(torch.mean, dim=0)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"reduce_op must be sum or avg\")\n    group = c10d._resolve_process_group(group_name)\n    out_shape = [*A.shape[:-1], B.shape[1]]\n    out_shape[scatter_dim] //= group.size()\n\n    if scatter_dim == A.ndim - 1:\n        B_shards = B.chunk(group.size(), dim=B.ndim - 1)\n        A_flat = A.flatten(0, -2)\n\n        def _chunk_producer(rank: int, out: torch.Tensor) -> None:\n            mm_out_op(A_flat, B_shards[rank], **kwargs, out=out)\n\n        leading_dims = list(A.shape[:-1])\n\n        stacked_partials = torch.empty(\n            (A_flat.shape[0], B.shape[1]),\n            dtype=out_dtype or A.dtype,\n )\n\n        stacked_partials_view = stacked_partials.reshape(\n            *leading_dims, group.size(), -1\n        )\n        return reduce_fn(\n            stacked_partials_view,\n            dim=-2,\n        )\n\n    # Move the scatter_dim to the front and flatten the tensor into a 2D matrix\n    x = A.movedim(scatter_dim, 0)\n    leading_dims = [group.size()] + list(x.shape[:-1])\n    leading_dims[1] //= group.size()\n    x = x.flatten(0, -2)\n    A_shards = x.chunk(group.size())\n\n    # Computing block-wise matmul along the first dim of A\n    def chunk_producer(rank: int, out: torch.Tensor) -> None:\n        mm_out_op(A_shards[rank], B, **kwargs, out=out)\n\n    stacked_partials = x.new_empty(x.shape[0], B.shape[1], dtype=out_dtype or A.dtype)\n\n    _pipelined_produce_and_all2all(\n        chunk_producer,\n        stacked_partials,\n        group_name,\n    )\n\n    # Ensures that the transpose and reduction produce contiguous result\n    # in a single reduction kernel.\n    return reduce_fn(\n        stacked_partials.view(*leading_dims, -1)\n        .movedim(1, scatter_dim + 1)\n        .movedim(0, scatter_dim),\n        dim=scatter_dim,\n    )\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter\", \"CUDA\")\ndef _fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter(\n    A: torch.Tensor,\n    B: torch.Tensor,\n    A_scale: torch.Tensor,\n    B_scale: torch.Tensor,\n    reduce_op: str,\n    orig_scatter_dim: int,\n    scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape: int,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n    output_shape: list[int],\n    bias:     return _fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter_fallback(\n            A,\n            B,\n            A_scale,\n            B_scale,\n            reduce_op,\n            orig_scatter_dim,\n            scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape,\n            group_name,\n            output_shape,\n            bias,\n            result_scale,\n            out_dtype,\n            use_fast_accum,\n        )\n    with torch.profiler.record_function(\"fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter\"):\n        return _fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter_impl(\n            mm_out_op=torch.ops.aten._scaled_mm.out,\n            A=A,\n            B=B,\n            A_scale=A_scale,\n            kwargs={\n                \"scale_b\": B_scale,\n                \"bias\": bias,\n                \"scale_result\": result_scale,\n                \"out_dtype\": out_dtype,\n                \"use_fast_accum\": use_fast_accum,\n            },\n            out_dtype=out_dtype,\n            reduce_op=reduce_op,\n            orig_scatter_dim=orig_scatter_dim,\n            scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape=scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape,\n            group_name=group_name,\n            output_shape=output_shape,\n        )\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter\", \"Meta\")\ndef _fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter_fallback(\n    A: torch.Tensor,\n    B: torch.Tensor,\n    A_scale: torch.Tensor,\n    B_scale: torch.Tensor,\n    reduce_op: str,\n    orig_scatter_dim: int,\n    scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape: int,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n    output_shape: list[int],\n    bias: torch.Tensor | None = None,\n    result_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,\n    out_dtype: torch.dtype | None = None,\n    use_fast_accum: bool = False,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    if A_scale.numel() > 1:\n        if A_scale.shape[:-1] != A.shape[:-1]:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"For row-wise scaling, the leading dims of A_scale \"\n                \"must match the leading dims of A \"\n                f\"(A shape: {A.shape}, A_scale shape: {A_scale.shape})\"\n            )\n        A_scale = A_scale.flatten(0, -2).contiguous()\n    elif A_scale.numel() != 1:\n        raise ValueError(\n            \"Invalid A_scale shape \"\n            f\"(A shape: {A.shape}, A_scale shape: {A_scale.shape})\"\n        )\n\n    C = torch._scaled_mm(\n        A.flatten(0, -2).contiguous(),\n        B,\n        A_scale,\n        B_scale,\n        bias,\n        result_scale,\n        out_dtype,\n        use_fast_accum,\n    )\n    C = C.view(*output_shape[:-1], B.shape[1])\n    res = funcol.reduce_scatter_tensor(\n        C,\n        reduce_op,\n        orig_scatter_dim,  # need original scatter dim for 3D+ output tensor here\n        group_name,\n    )\n    res = funcol.wait_tensor(res)\n    return res\n\n\ndef _fused_scaled_matmul_reduce_scatter_impl(\n    mm_out_op: torch._ops.OpOverload,\n    A: torch.Tensor,\n    B: torch.Tensor,\n    A_scale: torch.Tensor,\n    kwargs: dict[str, Any],\n    out_dtype: torch.dtype | None,\n    reduce_op: str,\n    orig_scatter_dim: int,\n    scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape: int,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n    output_shape: list[int],\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    if A.dim() < 2:\n        raise ValueError(\"A_shard must be a matrix\")\n    if (\n        scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape < 0\n        or scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape >= A.dim()\n    ):\n        raise ValueError(\"Invalid scatter dim for 2D tensor input to scaled_mm\")\n    if orig_scatter_dim < 0 or orig_scatter_dim >= len(output_shape):\n        raise ValueError(\"Invalid scatter dim for 3D+ output tensor\")\n    if B.dim() != 2:\n        raise ValueError(\"B must be a matrix\")\n    if reduce_op == \"sum\":\n        reduce_fn = partial(torch.sum, dim=0)\n    elif reduce_op == \"avg\":\n        reduce_fn = partial(torch.mean, dim=0)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"reduce_op must be sum or avg\")\n\n    group = c10d._resolve_process_group(group_name)\n\n    # Move scatter to first dim, then shard the tensor along the first dim, so the chunk producer\n    # can perform matmuls along the first dim.\n    A_with_scatter_dim_0 = A.movedim(scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape, 0)\n\n    # To handle case where A is 3D+, reshape to 2D to prepare for mm which requires 2D inputs.\n    A_2D_with_scatter_dim_0 = A_with_scatter_dim_0.flatten(0, -2)\n\n    # Partition A along the first dim to prepare for sharding across TP process group.\n    A_shards = A_2D_with_scatter_dim_0.chunk(group.size())\n\n    # Now that 'A' is sharded along the first dim, we need to update its scale(s) accordingly.\n    # How we do this depends on if we are using tensorwise scaling, rowwise scaling, or no scaling.\n    tensorwise_scaling = A_scale is not None and A_scale.numel() == 1\n    rowwise_scaling = A_scale is not None and A_scale.numel() > 1\n\n    # For tensorwise scaling, the scale should be replicated so each shard has a copy.\n    if tensorwise_scaling:\n        A_scale_shards = [A_scale] * group.size()\n\n    # For rowwise scaling, we need to move the scatter dim to the first dim to match the\n    # dim swap of the 'A' tensor. Then we can shard the scales along the first dim, just like\n    # the 'A' tensor.\n    elif rowwise_scaling:\n        if A_scale.shape[:-1] != A.shape[:-1]:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"For row-wise scaling, the leading dims of A_scale \"\n                \"must match the leading dims of A \"\n                f\"(A shape: {A.shape}, A_scale shape: {A_scale.shape})\"\n            )\n        A_scale = (\n            A_scale.movedim(scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape, 0)\n            .contiguous()\n            .flatten(0, -2)\n        )\n        A_scale_shards = list(A_scale.chunk(group.size()))\n        # cuBLAS's row-wise kernel requires scales to be aligned to 16 bytes.\n        # When we slice them we might break this and need to reallocate them.\n        A_scale_shards = [\n            t if t.data_ptr() % 16 == 0 else t.clone() for t in A_scale_shards\n        ]\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"A_scale cannot be none for scaled_mm\")\n\n    # Computing block-wise matmul along the first dim of A\n    def chunk_producer(rank: int, out: torch.Tensor) -> None:\n        mm_out_op(A_shards[rank], B, scale_a=A_scale_shards[rank], **kwargs, out=out)\n\n    # Stacked partials will be the 2D outputs of the pipelined scaled mm, and will\n    # have the shape (A_with_scatter_dim_0_tensor.shape[0], B.shape[1]) to align with the formula:\n    # (a*b,c) @ (c,d) = (a*b,d)\n    stacked_partials = A_with_scatter_dim_0.new_empty(\n        A_2D_with_scatter_dim_0.shape[0], B.shape[1], dtype=out_dtype or A.dtype\n    )\n\n    # Execute the pipelined mm/scaled_mm.\n    _pipelined_produce_and_all2all(\n        chunk_producer,\n        stacked_partials,\n        group_name,\n    )\n\n    # We now need to transform the *unreduced* stacked 2D partial mm outputs to an *unreduced* 3D+ output,\n    # then reduce-scatter. To do this, we first need to determine the shape of the unreduced 3D+ output,\n    # to reshape our stacked partials so we can apply the reduce-scatter.\n    #\n    # The *unreduced* 3D+ tensor will have dim 0 = `group_size`, as we have `group_size` instances of\n    # stacked partial outputs. The next dims will be A's leading dims (sharded along the original scatter dim),\n    # as it was the left operand of the mm op. We can use -1 as the final dim of the view to populate the rest.\n    stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims = [group.size()] + list(\n        # We use A from after the dim swap 0<=>scatter_dim, but before the flatten,\n        # to get the leading dims of the 3D+ view of stacked partials.\n        A_with_scatter_dim_0.shape[:-1]\n    )\n\n    # The `group_size` leading dim has been prepended to `stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims`,\n    # to capture the partial output from each rank. We need to divide the sharding/scatter dim\n    # by the group size. If the original scatter dim was 0, then it is now dim 1 in this\n    # tensor, since this new `group_size` dim was prepended.\n    stacked_partial_scatter_dim = orig_scatter_dim if orig_scatter_dim > 0 else 1\n    stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims[stacked_partial_scatter_dim] //= group.size()\n\n    # Ensures that the transpose and reduction produce contiguous result\n    # in a single reduction kernel.\n    reduced_out = reduce_fn(\n        # View 2D stacked partials as 3D+ tensor of shape (`group_size`, ...)\n        stacked_partials.view(*stacked_partials_3D_leading_dims, -1)\n        # We originally swapped 0<=>scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape. Now after\n        # prepending the `group_size` dim, to undo this original swap, we\n        # must swap 1<=>scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape+1.\n        .movedim(1, scatter_dim_after_maybe_reshape + 1),\n        # Reduce along the `group_size` dim (0).\n        dim=0,\n    )\n\n    # Output shape must be scattered along original scatter dim as well.\n    output_shape[orig_scatter_dim] //= group.size()\n    out = reduced_out.view(*output_shape)\n    return out\n\n\ndef restride_A_for_fused_matmul_reduce_scatter(\n    t: torch.Tensor,\n    scatter_dim: int,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"\n    Restride the `A_shard` arg of `fused_matmul_reduce_scatter` for optimal\n    perf. See the doc for `fused_matmul_reduce_scatter` for detail.\n    \"\"\"\n    perm = list(range(len(t.shape)))\n    perm.insert(0, perm.pop(scatter_dim))\n    return make_contiguous_for_perm(t, perm)\n\n\ndef _maybe_convert_scalar_types_to_dtypes(\n    scalar_types: list[Any],\n) -> list[torch.dtype | None]:\n    \"\"\"\n    When a list of `torch.dtype`s is passed through the dispatcher as\n    `ScalarType[]`, it is converted to a list of scalar type enum values. This\n    function converts it back to a list of `torch.dtype`s.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Order defined in https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/344defc9733a45fee8d0c4d3f5530f631e823196/c10/core/ScalarType.h\n    _SCALAR_TYPE_TO_DTYPE = {\n        0: torch.uint8,\n        1: torch.int8,\n        2: torch.short,\n        3: torch.int,\n        4: torch.int64,\n        5: torch.half,\n        6: torch.float,\n        7: torch.double,\n        8: torch.complex32,\n        9: torch.complex64,\n        10: torch.complex128,\n        11: torch.bool,\n        12: torch.qint8,\n        13: torch.quint8,\n        14: torch.qint32,\n        15: torch.bfloat16,\n        16: torch.float8_e5m2,\n        17: torch.float8_e4m3fn,\n        18: torch.float8_e5m2fnuz,\n        19: torch.float8_e4m3fnuz,\n    }\n    if any(not isinstance(x, (type(None), int)) for x in scalar_types):\n        return scalar_types\n\n    dtypes: list[torch.dtype | None] = []\n    for scalar_type in scalar_types:\n        if scalar_type is None:\n            dtypes.append(scalar_type)\n        elif scalar_type not in _SCALAR_TYPE_TO_DTYPE:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Unrecognized scalar type {scalar_type}\")\n        else:\n            dtypes.append(_SCALAR_TYPE_TO_DTYPE[scalar_type])\n    return dtypes\n\n\nclass Work(_Work):\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        super().__init__()\n        self.event = torch.cuda.Event()\n        self.event.record()\n\n    def wait(self, timeout: timedelta = timedelta(seconds=0)) -> bool:\n        self.event.wait()\n        return True\n\n\n\"\"\"\nNOTE [low-contention collectives]\nWhen a collective is overlapped with abundant compute, it makes sense to\nprioritize reducing the contention between the collective and the overlapped\ncompute, even at the cost of a slightly slower collective.\n\nCommon collective implementations (e.g., NCCL without user buffer\nregistration) optimize for throughput with no ambient compute. However, such\nimplementations may not be optimal when they are overlapped with compute:\n- These implementations typically fuse the entire collective into a single\nkernel and reserve SM resources based on the most demanding portion of the\ncollective, even when a large portion of the collective does not require this\nmuch resource.\n- These implementations often use SM-based P2P copy as opposed to copy\nengine-based P2P copy. Copy engine-based P2P copy may not have a significant\nadvantage when there's no ambient compute. However, it may significantly\nimprove overall resource utilization in the presence of ambient compute.\n\nWhen overlapped with intensive compute (e.g., persistent matmul kernels), the\nSM-usage of a collective can lead to inefficient overlapping.\n\nLow-contention collectives achieve their goals with the following strategies:\n- Use copy engine-based copy whenever possible.\n- Break down portions of a collective with different resource requirements\ninto multiple kernels. This improves the overlapping efficiency at the cost\nof additional launching overhead.\n\"\"\"\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_all_gather\", \"Meta\")\ndef _low_contention_all_gather_meta(\n    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    group_size = c10d._get_group_size_by_name(group_name)\n    return tensor.new_empty(tensor.shape[0] * group_size, *tensor.shape[1:])\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_all_gather\", \"CUDA\")\ndef _low_contention_all_gather(\n    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"\n    Performs all-gather with symmetric memory in a low-contention fashion.\n\n    When `tensor` is already in symmetric memory:\n        - The collective is carried out without using SMs.\n        - No symmetric memory workspace is required.\n\n    When `tensor` is not in symmetric memory:\n        - An extra SM-based copy is performed to copy the input data into the\n          symmetric memory workspace.\n        - Symmetric memory workspace size requirement: the size of `tensor`.\n    \"\"\"\n    symm_mem = rendezvous(tensor, group_name)\n    if symm_mem is not None:\n        input_is_symm_mem = True\n    else:\n        symm_mem = get_symm_mem_workspace(\n            group_name, tensor.numel() * tensor.element_size()\n        )\n        input_is_symm_mem = False\n\n    rank = symm_mem.rank\n    world_size = symm_mem.world_size\n\n    output = tensor.new_empty(tensor.shape[0] * world_size, *tensor.shape[1:])\n    chunks = output.chunk(world_size)\n\n    _get_backend_stream().wait_stream(torch.cuda.current_stream())\n    with _get_backend_stream():\n        if not input_is_symm_mem:\n            local_buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(rank, tensor.shape, tensor.dtype)\n            local_buf.copy_(tensor)\n        # pull\n        symm_mem.barrier()\n        for step in range(world_size):\n            remote_rank = (rank - step) % world_size\n            src_buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(remote_rank, tensor.shape, tensor.dtype)\n            chunks[remote_rank].copy_(src_buf)\n        symm_mem.barrier()\n        torch._C._distributed_c10d._register_work(output, Work())\n        return output\n\n\n@torch.library.impl(lib, \"_low_contention_reduce_scatter\", \"Meta\")\ndef _low_contention_reduce_scatter_meta(\n    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n    reduce_op: str,\n    group_name: c10d.GroupName,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    group_size = c10d._get_group_size_by_name(group_name)\n    return tensor.unflatten(0, (group_size, -1)).mean(dim=0)\n\n\ndef _low_contention_reduce_scatter_with_symm_mem_input(\n    tensor: torch.Tensor,\n    reduce_op: str,\n    symm_mem: _SymmetricMemory,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    rank = symm_mem.rank\n    world_size = symm_mem.world_size\n\n    assert tensor.shape[0] % world_size == 0\n    a2a_res = torch.empty_like(tensor)\n    chunks = a2a_res.chunk(world_size)\n\n    _get_backend_stream().wait_stream(torch.cuda.current_stream())\n    with _get_backend_stream():\n        # pull + offline reduction\n        symm_mem.barrier()\n        for step in range(world_size):\n            remote_rank = (rank - step) % world_size\n            src_buf = symm_mem.get_buffer(\n                remote_rank,\n                chunks[0].shape,\n                chunks[0].dtype,\n                chunks[0].numel() * rank,\n            )\n            chunks[remote_rank].copy_(src_buf)\n        symm_mem.barrier()\n\n        ret = a2a_res.unflatten(0,   tensor: torch.Tensor,\n    reduce_op: str,\n    workspace: _SymmetricMemory,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    rank = workspace.rank\n    world_size = workspace.world_size\n\n    assert tensor.shape[0] % -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"\n    Performs reduce-scatter with symmetric memory in a low-contention fashion.\n\n    This implementation performs a P2P-based all-to-all followed by an offline\n    reduction.\n\n    When `tensor` is already in symmetric memory:\n        - Pull-based all-to-all is used.\n        - No symmetric memory workspace is required.\n\n    When `tensor` is not in symmetric The table below shows which functions are available\n    25→for use with a CPU or GPU for each backend. For NCCL, GPU refers to CUDA GPU\n    26→while for XCCL to XPU GPU.\n    27→\n    28→MPI supports CUDA only if the implementation used to build PyTorch supports it.\n    29→\n    30→```{eval-rst}\n    31→+----------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+\n    32→| Backend        | ``gloo``  | ``mpi``   | ``nccl``  | ``xccl``  |\n    33→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    34→| Device         | CPU | GPU | CPU | GPU | CPU | GPU | CPU | GPU |\n    35→+================+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+\n    36→| send           | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    37→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    38→| recv           | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    39→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    40→| broadcast      | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    41→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    42→| all_reduce     | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    43→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    44→| reduce         | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    45→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    46→| all_gather     | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    47→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    48→| gather         | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    49→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    50→| scatter        | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    51→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    52→| reduce_scatter | ✓   | ✓   | ✘   | ✘   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    53→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    54→| all_to_all     | ✘   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    55→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    56→| barrier        | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n    57→+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n    58→```\n    59→\n    60→### Backends that come with PyTorch\n    61→\n    62→PyTorch distributed package supports Linux (stable), macOS (stable), and Windows (prototype).\n    63→By default for Linux, the Gloo and NCCL backends are built and included in PyTorch\n    64→distributed (NCCL only when building with CUDA). MPI is an optional backend that can only be\n    65→included if you build PyTorch from source. (e.g. building PyTorch on a host that has MPI\n    66→installed.)\n    67→\n    68→:::{note}\n    69→As of PyTorch v1.8, Windows supports all collective communications backends but NCCL,\n    70→If the `init_method` argument of {func}`init_process_group` points to a file it must adhere\n    71→to the following schema:\n    72→\n    73→- Local file system, `init_method=\"file:///d:/tmp/some_file\"`\n    74→- Shared file system, `init_method=\"file://////{machine_name}/{share_folder_name}/some_file\"`\n    75→\n    76→Same as on Linux platform, you can enable TcpStore by setting environment variables,\n    77→MASTER_ADDR and MASTER_PORT.\n    78→:::\n    79→\n    80→### Which backend to use?\n    81→\n    82→In the past, we were often asked: \"which backend should I use?\".\n    83→\n    84→- Rule of thumb\n    85→\n    86→  - Use the NCCL backend for distributed training with CUDA **GPU**.\n    87→  - Use the XCCL backend for distributed training with XPU **GPU**.\n    88→  - Use the Gloo backend for distributed training with **CPU**.\n    89→\n    90→- GPU hosts with InfiniBand interconnect\n    91→\n    92→  - Use NCCL, since it's the only backend that currently supports\n    93→    InfiniBand and GPUDirect.\n    94→\n    95→- GPU hosts with Ethernet interconnect\n    96→\n    97→  - Use NCCL, since it currently provides the best distributed GPU\n    98→    training performance, especially for multiprocess single-node or\n    99→    multi-node distributed training. If you encounter any problem with\n   100→    NCCL, use Gloo as the fallback option. (Note that Gloo currently\n   101→    runs slower than NCCL for GPUs.)\n   102→\n   103→- CPU hosts with InfiniBand interconnect\n   104→\n   105→  - If your InfiniBand has enabled IP over IB, use Gloo, otherwise,\n   106→    use MPI instead. We are planning on adding InfiniBand support for\n   107→    Gloo in the upcoming releases.\n   108→\n   109→- CPU hosts with Ethernet interconnect\n   110→\n   111→  - Use Gloo, unless you have specific reasons to use MPI.\n   112→\n   113→### Common environment variables\n   114→\n   115→#### Choosing the network interface to use\n   116→\n   117→By default, both the NCCL and Gloo backends will try to find the right network interface to use.\n   118→If the automatically detected interface is not correct, you can override it using the following\n   119→environment variables (applicable to the respective backend):\n   120→\n   121→- **NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME**, for example `export NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME=eth0`\n   122→- **GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME**, for example `export GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME=eth0`\n   123→\n   124→If you're using the Gloo backend, you can specify multiple interfaces by separating\n   125→them by a comma, like this: `export GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME=eth0,eth1,eth2,eth3`.\n   126→The backend will dispatch operations in a round-robin fashion across these interfaces.\n   127→It is imperative that all processes specify the same number of interfaces in this variable.\n   128→\n   129→#### Other NCCL environment variables\n   130→\n   131→**Debugging** - in case of NCCL failure, you can set `NCCL_DEBUG=INFO` to print an explicit\n   132→warning message as well as basic NCCL initialization information.\n   133→\n   134→You may also use `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS` to get more details about a specific\n   135→aspect of NCCL. For example, `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS=COLL` would print logs of\n   136→collective calls, which may be helpful when debugging hangs, especially those\n   137→caused by collective type or message size mismatch. In case of topology\n   138→detection failure, it would be helpful to set `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS=GRAPH`\n   139→to inspect the detailed detection result and save as reference if further help\n   140→from NCCL team is needed.\n   141→\n   142→**Performance tuning** - NCCL performs automatic tuning based on its topology detection to save users'\n   143→tuning effort. On some socket-based systems, users may still try tuning\n   144→`NCCL_SOCKET_NTHREADS` and `NCCL_NSOCKS_PERTHREAD` to increase socket\n   145→network bandwidth. These two environment variables have been pre-tuned by NCCL\n   146→for some cloud providers, such as AWS or GCP.\n   147→\n   148→For a full list of NCCL environment variables, please refer to\n   149→[NVIDIA NCCL's official documentation](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/nccl/user-guide/docs/env.html)\n   150→\n   151→You can tune NCCL communicators even further using `torch.distributed.ProcessGroupNCCL.NCCLConfig`\n   152→and `torch.distributed.ProcessGroupNCCL.Options`. Learn more about them using `help`\n   153→(e.g. `help(torch.distributed.ProcessGroupNCCL.NCCLConfig)`) in the interpreter.\n   154→\n   155→(distributed-basics)=\n   156→\n   157→## Basics\n   158→\n   159→The `torch.distributed` package provides PyTorch support and communication primitives\n   160→for multiprocess parallelism across several computation nodes running on one or more\n   161→machines. The class {func}`torch.nn.parallel.DistributedDataParallel` builds on this\n   162→functionality to provide synchronous distributed training as a wrapper around any\n   163→PyTorch model. This differs from the kinds of parallelism provided by\n   164→{doc}`multiprocessing` and {func}`torch.nn.DataParallel` in that it supports\n   165→multiple network-connected machines and in that the user must explicitly launch a separate\n   166→copy of the main training script for each process.\n   167→\n   168→In the single-machine synchronous case, `torch.distributed` or the\n   169→{func}`torch.nn.parallel.DistributedDataParallel` wrapper may still have advantages over other\n   170→approaches to data-parallelism, including {func}`torch.nn.DataParallel`:\n   171→\n   172→- Each process maintains its own optimizer and performs a complete optimization step with each\n   173→  iteration. While this may appear redundant, since the gradients have already been gathered\n   174→  together and averaged across processes and are thus the same for every process, this means\n   175→  that no parameter broadcast step is needed, reducing time spent transferring tensors between\n   176→  nodes.\n   177→- Each process contains an independent Python interpreter, eliminating the extra interpreter\n   178→  overhead and \"GIL-thrashing\" that comes from driving several execution threads, model\n   179→  replicas, or GPUs from a single Python process. This is especially important for models that\n   180→  make heavy use of the Python runtime, including models with recurrent layers or many small\n   181→  components.\n   182→\n   183→## Initialization\n   184→\n   185→The package needs to be initialized using the {func}`torch.distributed.init_process_group`\n   186→or {func}`torch.distributed.device_mesh.init_device_mesh` function before calling any other methods.\n   187→Both block until all processes have joined.\n   188→\n   189→:::{warning}\n   190→Initialization is not thread-safe. The table below shows which functions are available\nfor use with a CPU or GPU for each backend. For NCCL, GPU refers to CUDA GPU\nwhile for XCCL to XPU GPU.\n\nMPI supports CUDA only if the implementation used to build PyTorch supports it.\n\n```{eval-rst}\n+----------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+\n| Backend        | ``gloo``  | ``mpi``   | ``nccl``  | ``xccl``  |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| Device         | CPU | GPU | CPU | GPU | CPU | GPU | CPU | GPU |\n+================+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+=====+\n| send           | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| recv           | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| broadcast      | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| all_reduce     | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| reduce         | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| all_gather     | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| gather         | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| scatter        | ✓   | ✓   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| reduce_scatter | ✓   | ✓   | ✘   | ✘   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| all_to_all     | ✘   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n| barrier        | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   | ?   | ✘   | ✓   | ✘   | ✓   |\n+----------------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+\n```\n\n### Backends that come with PyTorch\n\nPyTorch distributed package supports Linux (stable), macOS (stable), and Windows (prototype).\nBy default for Linux, the Gloo and NCCL backends are built and included in PyTorch\ndistributed (NCCL only when building with CUDA). MPI is an optional backend that can only be\nincluded if you build PyTorch from source. (e.g. building PyTorch on a host that has MPI\ninstalled.)\n\n:::{note}\nAs of PyTorch v1.8, Windows supports all collective communications backends but NCCL,\nIf the `init_method` argument of {func}`init_process_group` points to a file it must adhere\nto the following schema:\n\n- Local file system, `init_method=\"file:///d:/tmp/some_file\"`\n- Shared file system, `init_method=\"file://////{machine_name}/{share_folder_name}/some_file\"`\n\nSame as on Linux platform, you can enable TcpStore by setting environment variables,\nMASTER_ADDR and MASTER_PORT.\n:::\n\n### Which backend to use?\n\nIn the past, we were often asked: \"which backend should I use?\".\n\n- Rule of thumb\n\n  - Use the NCCL backend for distributed training with CUDA **GPU**.\n  - Use the XCCL backend for distributed training with XPU **GPU**.\n  - Use the Gloo backend for distributed training with **CPU**.\n\n- GPU hosts with InfiniBand interconnect\n\n  - Use NCCL, since it's the only backend that currently supports\n    InfiniBand and GPUDirect.\n\n- GPU hosts with Ethernet interconnect\n\n  - Use NCCL, since it currently provides the best distributed GPU\n    training performance, especially for multiprocess single-node or\n    multi-node distributed training. If you encounter any problem with\n    NCCL, use Gloo as the fallback option. (Note that Gloo currently\n    runs slower than NCCL for GPUs.)\n\n- CPU hosts with InfiniBand interconnect\n\n  - If your InfiniBand has enabled IP over IB, use Gloo, otherwise,\n    use MPI instead. We are planning on adding InfiniBand support for\n    Gloo in the upcoming releases.\n\n- CPU hosts with Ethernet interconnect\n\n  - Use Gloo, unless you have specific reasons to use MPI.\n\n### Common environment variables\n\n#### Choosing the network interface to use\n\nBy default, both the NCCL and Gloo backends will try to find the right network interface to use.\nIf the automatically detected interface is not correct, you can override it using the following\nenvironment variables (applicable to the respective backend):\n\n- **NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME**, for example `export NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME=eth0`\n- **GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME**, for example `export GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME=eth0`\n\nIf you're using the Gloo backend, you can specify multiple interfaces by separating\nthem by a comma, like this: `export GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME=eth0,eth1,eth2,eth3`.\nThe backend will dispatch operations in a round-robin fashion across these interfaces.\nIt is imperative that all processes specify the same number of interfaces in this variable.\n\n#### Other NCCL environment variables\n\n**Debugging** - in case of NCCL failure, you can set `NCCL_DEBUG=INFO` to print an explicit\nwarning message as well as basic NCCL initialization information.\n\nYou may also use `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS` to get more details about a specific\naspect of NCCL. For example, `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS=COLL` would print logs of\ncollective calls, which may be helpful when debugging hangs, especially those\ncaused by collective type or message size mismatch. In case of topology\ndetection failure, it would be helpful to set `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS=GRAPH`\nto inspect the detailed detection result and save as reference if further help\nfrom NCCL team is needed.\n\n**Performance tuning** - NCCL performs automatic tuning based on its topology detection to save users'\ntuning effort. On some socket-based systems, users may still try tuning\n`NCCL_SOCKET_NTHREADS` and `NCCL_NSOCKS_PERTHREAD` to increase socket\nnetwork bandwidth. These two environment variables have been pre-tuned by NCCL\nfor some cloud providers, such as AWS or GCP.\n\nFor a full list of NCCL environment variables, please refer to\n[NVIDIA NCCL's official documentation](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/nccl/user-guide/docs/env.html)\n\nYou can tune NCCL communicators even further using `torch.distributed.ProcessGroupNCCL.NCCLConfig`\nand `torch.distributed.ProcessGroupNCCL.Options`. Learn more about them using `help`\n(e.g. `help(torch.distributed.ProcessGroupNCCL.NCCLConfig)`) in the interpreter.\n\n(distributed-basics)=\n\n## Basics\n\nThe `torch.distributed` package provides PyTorch support and communication primitives\nfor multiprocess parallelism across several computation nodes running on one or more\nmachines. The class {func}`torch.nn.parallel.DistributedDataParallel` builds on this\nfunctionality to provide synchronous distributed training as a wrapper around any\nPyTorch model. This differs from the kinds of parallelism provided by\n{doc}`multiprocessing` and {func}`torch.nn.DataParallel` in that it supports\nmultiple network-connected machines and in that the user must explicitly launch a separate\ncopy of the main training script for each process.\n\nIn the single-machine synchronous case, `torch.distributed` or the\n{func}`torch.nn.parallel.DistributedDataParallel` wrapper may still have advantages over other\napproaches to data-parallelism, including {func}`torch.nn.DataParallel`:\n\n- Each process maintains its own optimizer and performs a complete optimization step with each\n  iteration. While this may appear redundant, since the gradients have already been gathered\n  together and averaged across processes and are thus the same for every process, this means\n  that no parameter broadcast step is needed, reducing time spent transferring tensors between\n  nodes.\n- Each process contains an independent Python interpreter, eliminating the extra interpreter\n  overhead and \"GIL-thrashing\" that comes from driving several execution threads, model\n  replicas, or GPUs from a single Python process. This is especially important for models that\n  make heavy use of the Python runtime, including models with recurrent layers or many small\n  components.\n\n## Initialization\n\nThe package needs to be initialized using the {func}`torch.distributed.init_process_group`\nor {func}`torch.distributed.device_mesh.init_device_mesh` function before calling any other methods.\nBoth block until all processes have joined.\n\n:::{warning}\nInitialization is not thread-safe. Process group creation should be performed from a single thread, to prevent\ninconsistent 'UUID' assignment across ranks, and to prevent races during initialization that can lead to hangs.\n:::\n\n```{eval-rst}\n.. autofunction:: is_available\n```\n\n```{eval-rst}\n.. autofunction:: init_process_group\n```","numLines":200,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1523}}}
